This is Codex TIER 2, round 3 of LHB-58, the last round: rounds 1 and 2 returned BLOCK, and if this round blocks, the branch stays unmerged.

The ticket, its pins and the builder decisions are in evidence/lhb-58-review-brief-r1.txt. Round 1's review is evidence/lhb-58-review-r1.txt and round 2's is evidence/lhb-58-review-r2.txt (its final message is at the end of the file). Round 2 findings:
1. P2 BLOCKING: onApplied repainted your Perfil only at #/mihoja/perfil; #/perfil (where deleting an uploaded cigar returns) shows the same Perfil and stayed stale.
2. P3: a repaint replaced every tab and review button, so keyboard focus fell to BODY and an open review sheet's return target (lastFocus) became a detached node. The HOJA58 harness masked this.

The fix (commit c5e45c3, site/hoja.js plus tests):
- onApplied: `{dKeepFocus();A.route()}else pfOwnSync()}`. The list pages go through the router as before. In every other case pfOwnSync runs; it already does nothing unless PFO's #pfOwn node is the one in the page and the same account is signed in, so it covers #/perfil, #/mihoja/perfil and any other route that mounts your Perfil, and it is a no-op elsewhere (including a list page with a sheet open).
- pfOwnPaint(o): before replacing the nodes it reads a key for document.activeElement and for lastFocus (pfKey): a tab by its id pfT-<tab>, or a review button by the review it shows (JSON of the projected entry in o.d, the data of the previous paint). After the paint it puts the focus back on the new button for that key (only if the focus was on one of them, so focus inside an open sheet is not stolen) and retargets lastFocus the same way (pfFind). A review that moved is found at its new place; a review that is gone falls back to the open tab. This runs on every paint, so the repaint after /api/u/ answers keeps the focus too.
- Harness HOJA58: a fake document.activeElement; focus() moves it; repainting #pfOwn or #pfX drops its nodes and stubs (a dropped focused node leaves the focus on BODY, as in a browser); repainting #pfPanel drops the review buttons; the sheet mock takes the focus and records the opener in lastFocus.
- Tests: a new test evaluates the onApplied callback exactly as written in hoja.js and checks both hashes repaint, the list page still routes, and no Perfil means no paint; a new test checks focus after the server answer and after a sync, the arrow keys still working, an open sheet returning to the same review after it moved from first to second, and the fallback to the open tab when the review is gone. The three pins of the onApplied line were updated.
Evidence: evidence/lhb-58-r2fix-tests-red.txt (the two new tests red before the fix, plus the three pins), evidence/lhb-58-r2fix-tests.txt (259 of 259), evidence/lhb-58-mutations-r2fix2.txt (32 of 32 red; N28 to N32 for the round 2 rules; evidence/lhb-58-mutations-r2fix.txt is the first run, where N21's anchor had moved).

+/* LHB-58: GET /api/u/<handle>, el perfil de una persona para la app (pasaporte primero). Pública, como /u/<handle> (LHB-40), y con la misma lectura: publicPassport.
+   → { profile: {handle, display_name, bio, favorite_brand, avatar_key}, counts: {resenas, followers, following, sellos: {artesanos, cigarros, lugares}}, resenas: [...] }
+   "resenas" son las catas que su dueño marcó públicas (estrellas, sabores, notas). Los seguidores y seguidos salen de la tabla follows (LHB-56), solo perfiles visibles.
+   Nada privado: ni user_id, ni el apellido completo, ni el género, ni la fecha de nacimiento, ni lo demás de Mi Hoja. Un perfil oculto o que no existe da 404. */
+import { json } from '../../_lib/auth.js';
+import { publicPassport } from '../../_lib/passport.js';
+import { countsOf } from '../../_lib/follow.js';
+
+export async function onRequestGet({ env, params }) {
+  if (!env.DB) return json({ error: 'no-db' }, 503);
+  try {
+    const r = await publicPassport(env.DB, params && params.handle);
+    if (!r) return json({ error: 'not-found' }, 404);
+    const f = await countsOf(env.DB, r.uid);
+    const { profile, counts, catas } = r.view;
+    return json({ profile, counts: { resenas: counts.catas, followers: f.followers, following: f.following, sellos: counts.sellos }, resenas: catas });
+  } catch (e) {
+    return json({ error: 'unavailable' }, 503);
+  }
+}

# PRE engine control methodology

Owner directive: Freddy, October 28, 2026. Applies to every operational workflow, including financial, project, document, communication and software work. This is standing guidance, not a replacement execution ledger or proof that an automated control has been implemented.

## Required operating method

1. **Identify.** Establish scope, account, canonical record, responsible owner and authority. Search for an existing obligation before creating a record. Preserve existing IDs and audience boundaries.
2. **Establish evidence.** Read the decisive source and current authoritative state. Record source IDs/links, source date, separate verification time, revision and file hash when relevant. Unknown or inaccessible evidence is NOT VERIFIED.
3. **Define the transition.** State the intended change, preconditions, acceptance criteria and recovery path. Separate permission to prepare from permission to send, pay, sign, publish or change access.
4. **Validate before execution.** Check identity, arithmetic, classification, dependencies, duplicates and applicable approvals. Engineering conclusions require their professional acceptance criteria. Failed checks block the affected transition.
5. **Execute within scope.** Use stable event IDs, idempotent operations, version/hash guards and atomic transactions where supported. After an uncertain result, inspect the saved state before retrying. Never replay a successful partial write.
6. **Verify independently.** Read back the authoritative saved record. Verify values, file bytes or hashes, links, parents, access, associations and downstream totals as relevant. Inspect rendered client documents. A success toast, a document number or an agent's statement is not proof of completion.
7. **Reconcile the durable tracker.** Update the existing execution record and affected projections using the same source event. Append history showing actor, source and recorded timestamps, prior/new state, evidence, owner and next action. Preserve manual annotations, originals and earlier responses. Do not create a competing tracker.
8. **Close or retain an owned exception.** Close only after acceptance checks and applicable writebacks pass. A failed or partial write stays visible in the existing outbox with an owner, next action and replay status. It cannot advance the successful checkpoint. Continue unrelated authorized work.

## Minimum persistent record

Each tracked obligation or material event needs project/item IDs, record type, provenance, owner (or explicitly unassigned), current status, prior status/history, source date, recorded time/timezone, dependency/next action, supported deadline or conditional trigger, closure criterion, verification proof, external system IDs and writeback/outbox state. Unknown values remain explicit; do not manufacture dates or owners.

Keep lifecycle states distinct: requested/proposed/authorized; prepared/draft/sent; received/reviewed/accepted; invoiced/credit-issued/credit-applied/part-paid/paid; reconciled/closed. A prepared document, receipt of a file or completion of one administrative step cannot establish later states.

## Controls by work type

| Work | Required controls |
| --- | --- |
| Billing and distributions | Executed scope and exact deliverable reference; cumulative versus incremental billing; prior invoices, deposits and distributions; separate fee, tax and credit; no duplicate charge, credit or payment; reconciliation to source ledgers; cash and accounting states kept separate. |
| Credits | Unique reference; original invoice/deposit provenance; fee/tax split; application capacity and residual balance; applications cannot exceed available credit; prepared, issued and posted states require separate proof. |
| Files and revisions | Preserve originals; compare hashes before duplicate-sensitive copying; verify canonical destination, revision, naming, parent IDs, access and index readback; archive without destroying provenance. |
| Communications | Correct account, language, recipients, thread, links and attachments; source-backed commitments; saved draft readback; follow the standing draft-only rule. |
| Engineering | Maintain professional ownership, scope and dependencies; distinguish received evidence from accepted design and issued work; retain review/signature gates and drawing content. |
| Code and deployment | Assigned code scope, current instructions, reviewable diff, relevant checks, recovery and verified target; a policy file is not runtime enforcement; separate deployment authority remains required. |
| Cross-system synchronization | One canonical execution authority; source-event deduplication; guarded writes; per-system readback; append-only history; owned partial-failure outbox; no false all-systems-current claim. |

## Engine enforcement and implementation gate

A spreadsheet can show a reconciliation without enforcing it. Do not describe procedural controls as SQL/database enforcement. Before adopting an engine, verify its canonical owner, schema, access, backups, transaction boundaries, unique IDs, state transitions, migration and recovery. Demonstrate duplicate retry, over-application rejection, partial-failure recovery and rollback with meaningful checks before cutover. Retain historical source values and parallel reconciliation until the cutover criteria pass.

For PDT Omelas, retain verified source -> existing mapped Linear issue -> Master Page evidence/history and current projection. Drive holds evidence and the existing financial workbook supplies visibility. SQL financial-posting enforcement is **OPEN / NOT IMPLEMENTED by this lane**, tracked under R13 / PDT-54. Do not create a parallel database, change authority, deploy, send, pay or expand access merely because this methodology exists. Repository/control publication remains with its assigned owner.

## Maintenance and proof

Update affected existing indexes, trackers and authorized records in the same task. Preserve restricted financial/legal material separately from shared technical records. A no-change scan creates no artificial progress event. Record each uncovered control gap with an owner or explicitly unassigned implementation owner, evidence, next action and closure criterion. A written rule's presence is verified separately from active-session adoption, automated execution and estate-wide coverage.

   [site/hoja.js:396](C:/Users/Owner/la-hoja-boricua/.claude/worktrees/lhb-40-58-profiles-5a5914/site/hoja.js:396), `pfKey` / `pfFind`: the key is `JSON.stringify(e)`, resolved with `findIndex(...)`.

   **Reproduction:** Create two public diary entries with different IDs but identical projected fields. Open the second review, apply a sync, then close the sheet. Chrome returns focus to review **0**, although review **1** still exists. Use local diary identity for focus tracking. This affects keyboard position only.

2. **[Certain] P3, nonblocking: the sheet mock still permits a broken focus implementation to pass.**  
   [test/site.test.js:4131](C:/Users/Owner/la-hoja-boricua/.claude/worktrees/lhb-40-58-profiles-5a5914/test/site.test.js:4131), `HOJA58`: `foco.el={id:'sheetPanel'}` always transfers focus. The real `sheet()` leaves focus on the opener for static review content.

   **Reproduction:** In memory, change `if(kf)` to `if(kf&&!document.body.classList.contains('sheet-on'))`. All **nine client-profile tests pass**, but Chrome drops focus to **BODY** when syncing with the review sheet open. Add that real-sheet case. This is a coverage gap; the submitted code does not contain that mutation.

[Certain] Round 2 dispositions:

- **#1 CONCUR, fixed:** `onApplied` refreshes both Perfil hashes. List routing remains intact. A stale or replaced `#pfOwn`, including a list with an open sheet, causes no repaint or storage read.
- **#2 CONCUR, fixed for the reported scenarios:** repaint preserves tab focus and keyboard navigation, retargets a moved review’s `lastFocus`, and falls back to the open tab when removed. Outside focus and return targets remain unchanged; sheet-close-button focus, account-change and sign-out guards passed.
